AI Agents in Biology: all 1235 papers

A curated, weekly updated collection of LLM-driven AI agents for biological and biomedical research, tagged by the five-dimension taxonomy of Qi et al., Briefings in Bioinformatics 2026. Updated 2026-10-23. † cited in the survey. Jump to 2026 · 2025 · 2024 · 2023.

Please cite: Qi C, Wang W, Jiang S, Liu Q, Song X, Fang H, Wei Z. Artificial Intelligence agents for biological research: a survey. Briefings in Bioinformatics 27(1), bbag075 (2026).

2026 (800)

Clinical decision support

  • An Evidence-Driven Multi-Agent Compound AI System for Head and Neck Proton Radiotherapy Planning
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Step-by-step, Hybrid; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    A reinforcement learning policy first generates candidate proton therapy plans, then LLM planning, chat and evaluation agents let physicians refine them conversationally with evidence from the reference plans. The planning agent justifies each recommended adjustment and the evaluation agent checks clinical metrics and constraint violations.

  • Agentic artificial intelligence for code status extraction from clinical notes: a cross-model comparison of single agent, reflective agent, and multiagent extractor-reviewer system
    2026-10 · Journal of the American Medical Informatics Association : JAMIA · Clinical decision support; Single-agent, Multi-agent, Neuro-symbolic; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric

    The paper evaluates different AI architectures for extracting clinical code status from discharge summaries, finding that single-agent systems perform comparably or better than more complex multiagent and reflective agent designs in capable models. The study highlights the importance of matching architectural complexity to model capability.

  • EchoMed Agent: EchoMed Agent: A Foundation Model–Based Agentic Framework for Echocardiographic Left Ventricular Segmentation and Functional Assessment
    2026-10 · Research Square · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    A constrained GPT-5.2 natural-language interface routes prompts to an adapted video segmentation foundation model and an axis-regression module to segment the left ventricle and estimate ejection fraction. With 20 labeled site-specific studies it reached a Dice of 0.940 and median ejection fraction error of 4.33 points on an independent Mayo Clinic cohort.

  • MedMCP: MedMCP: An Open Agentic Ecosystem for Medical Imaging Workflows
    2026-10 · bioRxiv · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    MedMCP is an agentic framework designed to make advanced medical image analysis tools accessible to non-technical users through natural language interaction. It demonstrates the potential for routine adoption of these methods by clinicians and researchers, enabling reproducible analyses with human oversight.

  • CS1: A general-purpose System One decision model for clinical records
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    CS1 is a 4-billion-parameter model that answers many typed questions about a patient record in one pass with calibrated probabilities, deployed inside two agentic harnesses alongside a generalist model. On 963,408 held-out patients it beat larger open decision models on 13 of 14 tasks and generalized across 21 external datasets.

  • Does Multi-Agent Collaboration Reduce Hallucination in Clinical Tasks?
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent, Single-agent; Human-in-the-loop

    The paper evaluates the impact of multi-agent collaboration on reducing hallucination in clinical tasks, finding that while collaboration improves task performance, it does not consistently reduce hallucination. The effectiveness varies by model rather than topology or number of agents.

  • LeakBench: LeakBench: Auditing Reward Hacking through Prediction-Time Leakage in Clinical Modeling Agents
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Human-in-the-loop, Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    LeakBench is a framework designed to audit clinical modeling agents for prediction-time leakage and other critical issues, using synthetic tasks and real-world datasets. It demonstrates that even advanced models can fail in specific conditions without proper constraints or oversight, highlighting the need for robust auditing mechanisms.

  • EGIP: EGIP: An Evidence-Grounded Interaction Protocol for Clinical Agents
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented

    EGIP is an interaction protocol designed to enhance the performance of clinical agents by enforcing structured evidence acquisition and decision-making processes. The system improves diagnostic accuracy across various datasets and language models, with clinician reviews indicating a significant increase in evidence-supported correct responses.

  • MedPAO-Assist: MedPAO-Assist: Edge-Deployable, Modality-Aware Agentic AI for Radiology Report Structuring
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent, Neuro-symbolic; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    MedPAO-Assist structures radiology reports from dictation on a laptop CPU using an extract-ground-generate pipeline with SNOMED-CT grounding and a 0.6B-parameter model. It covers four imaging modalities with about 3.3 GB peak memory and roughly 31 seconds end-to-end latency.

  • FusionRare: Unified Multiparadigm Fusion Intelligence for Rare Disease Diagnosis
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Multi-agent; Hybrid; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    FusionRare is a unified framework that integrates multiple AI paradigms to diagnose rare diseases, achieving superior diagnostic performance compared to existing methods. It demonstrates significant improvements in clinical decision-making by enhancing physician confidence and reducing diagnosis time.

  • inClinico Agent: inClinico Agent for clinical trial endpoint prediction and prospective evaluation
    2026-10 · medRxiv · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    The inClinico Agent is a machine-learning system that predicts the success of clinical trials based on registration-time features, providing a continuous Predictive Trial Index (PTI) for efficacy and safety/toxicity endpoints. The paper demonstrates high predictive accuracy with an AUROC of 0.828 for efficacy predictions and 0.809 for safety/toxicity predictions.

  • Auditing Escalation Reliability in Healthcare LLM Agents: A Perturbation-Controlled Benchmark of Workflow-State Failures
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Human-in-the-loop, Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    The paper evaluates the reliability of large language models in healthcare decision-making under uncertain conditions, using a synthetic benchmark with controlled perturbations. It highlights that while models perform well on clean data, their ability to escalate decisions when evidence is ambiguous or conflicting is significantly weaker.

  • The Model Knows Better: Defending Medical Multi-Agent Systems Against Prompt Injection
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Step-by-step, Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric

    The paper investigates the vulnerability of medical multi-agent systems to prompt injection attacks, where an attacker manipulates LLMs into incorrect decisions. It proposes a training-free defense mechanism that separates patient findings from clinical judgments and verifies each judgment against the findings alone, effectively reducing attack success rates while maintaining system accuracy.

  • TIR-EC: Incentivizing Tool-Use Reinforcement Learning for Post-Arrival Emergency Care Prediction
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent, Neuro-symbolic; Hybrid; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    TIR-EC is a reinforcement learning framework that integrates tools into large language models to improve their performance in emergency care prediction tasks. It demonstrates superior macro F1 scores compared to conventional ML methods and larger LLMs, while also showing more efficient use of clinical evidence.

  • TRACER: TRACER: A Multimodal Harness for Patient Care in Hospitals
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    TRACER is a multimodal system that integrates video and clinical records to provide minute-level action reports for patient care in hospitals. It demonstrates the ability to accurately detect patient actions and answer clinician questions, showing potential for supporting clinical decision-making without training on protected health data.

  • Towards guideline-grounded clinical decision-making: a multi-scale study of retrieval augmented generation based large language model agents
    2026-10 · Research Square · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    Evaluates retrieval-augmented agent configurations on 2,400 real MIMIC-CDM patient pathways across information gathering, diagnosis, treatment and guideline retrieval, with eight LLMs from 1B to 70B. Retrieval raised mean diagnostic accuracy from 55.6 to 64.3 percent, and autonomous query rewriting mattered most for retrieval fidelity.

  • Skill-guided agentic reasoning enables longitudinal synthesis of myeloma records at expert-level concordance
    2026-10 · NPJ precision oncology · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    An agent guided by an indexed clinical skill library synthesized longitudinal records of 811 myeloma patients and was compared against single-pass RAG, iterative RAG and full-context input. It reached 80.1% concordance with expert answers, above the roughly 76% ceiling of the alternatives, with larger gains on complex tasks.

  • HEM-Med: HEM-Med: A Hierarchical Agentic Memory Framework for Safe Multi-Center Medication Recommendation
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    HEM-Med is a hierarchical agentic memory framework designed for multi-center medication recommendation, addressing the challenge of transferring knowledge across hospitals while ensuring safety constraints. The system demonstrates improved performance in terms of both accuracy and safety compared to existing methods on real-world data.

  • PRISMATIC: PRISMATIC : Prescription Risk Inspection System for Multi-Agent Treatment Interaction in Clinical Decision-Making
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    PRISMATIC has specialty agents build and revise drug regimens for multimorbid patients on a blackboard, with agent influence weights adapted from past case outcomes and short-term discrepancy feedback. It targets prescribing errors that come from fragmented decisions across specialties.

  • Verification Turns Capability Gaps into Routing Decisions: Small Language Models for Clinical Documentation
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    This study fixes a four-evaluator verification stack and refinement policy for clinical note generation and swaps only the generator between a 4B on-device model and a frontier baseline. The small model reached 0.984 claim-level factual entailment against 0.992, with its remaining weakness in section arrangement turned into an explicit escalation decision.

  • TrialPD-Bench: The Last Mile of Coding Agents for Long-Document Tasks: An Empirical Study on Clinical Trial Protocol Digitization
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    TrialPD-Bench is an expert-annotated benchmark of 150 clinical trial protocol PDFs across four task categories for coding agents that select tools, run code and revise their approach. Across nine models in five harnesses, agents usually found the right evidence but failed at transcribing it into accurate structured output.

  • Reinforcement Learning In Partially Verifiable Environments for Colon Cancer Treatment Planning
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    Oncology treatment planning is cast as an interactive reinforcement learning problem under partial observability, with process-aware rewards built from decision sequences in real colon cancer records. The agent must decide what information to gather before committing to a treatment, a setting where several trajectories can be defensible.

  • ProCARE: ProCARE: Real-World Study Automation via Profile-Grounded Evidence Contracts
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    ProCARE is a framework that automates real-world studies by converting data profiles into Evidence Contracts and compiling study objectives into an intermediate representation, ensuring semantic consistency and traceability. It outperforms existing large language model agents in code executability and conclusion traceability on benchmark tasks, with validation through automated checks and expert review.

  • KGR-MAS: KGR-MAS: Knowledge Graph-Enhanced Multi-Agent LLM Framework for Evidence-Based Medical Diagnosis and Clinical Decision Support
    2026-10 · IEEE journal of biomedical and health informatics · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Multi-agent, Neuro-symbolic; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    KGR-MAS is a multi-agent framework that uses knowledge graphs to enhance the reasoning and decision-making capabilities of large language models in medical settings. It demonstrates improved accuracy on clinical diagnostic tasks compared to larger, less specialized models.

  • LongCAF: LongCAF: A longitudinal context-constructive agentic framework for LLM-supported hypertension self-management
    2026-10 · Journal of biomedical informatics · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    LongCAF is a longitudinal context-constructive agentic framework designed to support hypertension self-management by integrating patient-specific context and interaction coordination. The system was evaluated in a real-world deployment over six months, demonstrating improved context completeness and high precision in safety escalation.

  • ActiveMedAgent: ActiveMedAgent: Cost-Aware Trajectory Learning for Multimodal Medical Diagnosis
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric

    ActiveMedAgent is a framework that uses a cost-aware sequential logic to guide the acquisition of medical evidence, aiming to minimize costs while maximizing diagnostic accuracy. The system demonstrates its effectiveness by outperforming full-modality baselines in several benchmarks, highlighting its ability to identify when less information can lead to correct diagnoses.

  • MedZERO: MedZERO: Self-Evolving Agents for Open-Ended Medical Reasoning Through Controlled Knowledge Accumulation
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    MedZERO is a self-evolving framework designed for medical reasoning that combines an Examiner and a Reasoner to generate and solve complex medical questions, respectively. The system demonstrates significant improvements in accuracy on public medical reasoning benchmarks compared to existing methods.

  • Planner-Executor Style Multimodal Agentic System to Answer Patient Questions in Lung Cancer Screening CT
    2026-10 · medRxiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Human-in-the-loop, Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    The system uses a multi-agent architecture to answer patient questions about lung cancer screening CT results, demonstrating high accuracy in tool invocation and self-regulation. It was evaluated by physicians for clinical quality and patient understandability.

  • An agentic AI- and n8n automation-powered maternal care framework with predictive pregnancy monitoring and continuous learning
    2026-10 · Frontiers in digital health · Clinical decision support; Single-agent, Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    The Agentic AI Maternal Triage Framework uses predictive models and a retrieval-augmented generation-powered LLM to provide real-time risk stratification for maternal care, reducing alert fatigue in clinicians. The framework demonstrated high accuracy in simulation-based evaluations but requires further clinical validation before deployment.

  • PsyCIDRA: PsyCIDRA: A Dual-Agent Framework for Psychiatric Interviewing and Diagnostic Reasoning
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    PsyCIDRA pairs an interviewer agent that keeps working notes, loads expert skills and consults ICD-11 with a reasoning agent that reports hypotheses plus supporting, conflicting and missing evidence. It agreed with psychologists on whether to offer a hypothesis in 79.6% of blinded cases against 65.4% for direct prompting.

  • MedPrune: MedPrune: Topology-Efficient Multimodal Multi-Agent Communication Evolution for Medical VQA Tasks
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    MedPrune treats medical visual question answering as a communication graph of department specialist agents and learns to prune both irrelevant agents and low-value connections. The sparsification is meant to improve reasoning while cutting the token cost of redundant agent chatter.

  • MedicalHarness: MedicalHarness: A Controlled Evaluation of LLMs and Agent Harnesses on Medical Tasks
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    MedicalHarness evaluates the performance of large language models on medical tasks using different agent harnesses, revealing that the choice of harness significantly impacts outcomes. The study also identifies specific mechanisms within these harnesses that contribute to model performance.

  • LifeLong Digital Twin: LifeLong Digital Twin: A Unified Modeling Paradigm and Agent Harness for Event-Driven Lifelong Health State Trajectories
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    The LifeLong Digital Twin system models a person's lifelong health trajectory by integrating various types of data into daily health states and lifelong health contexts. It demonstrates significant improvements in predicting disease trajectories and outcomes compared to existing methods, highlighting its potential for personalized healthcare.

  • ARISE: ARISE: Adaptive Agentic Reasoning with Image-grounded Self-Evaluation for Interpretable IBD Assessment
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented

    ARISE is an autonomous planning framework designed to improve the interpretability and diagnostic performance of medical image analysis in inflammatory bowel disease (IBD). It demonstrates consistent improvement over baseline vision-language models by providing a transparent, step-by-step workflow that enhances clinical decision support.

  • Development and Validation of Human-AI Collaborative Workflow in SNOMED CT Mapping of Bilingual Clinical Text
    2026-10 · Journal of medical systems · Clinical decision support; Single-agent, Multi-agent; Hybrid; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented

    The paper describes a human-AI collaborative workflow for mapping bilingual clinical text to SNOMED CT concepts, using an agent system with translation, abbreviation expansion, and vector-based retrieval modules. The study shows that the AI-assisted approach improves mapping accuracy and reduces time by about half compared to human-only or AI-only methods.

  • Sentinel: Trajectory-Derived Confidence for Reliable, Resource-Aware Clinical Text-to-SQL Agents
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    Sentinel is a confidence layer that reads a clinical text-to-SQL agent's reasoning, code and database outputs to refuse unanswerable questions, abort failing trajectories and withhold untrustworthy answers. On EHRSQL it raised the reliability score from +0.08 to +0.24 and delivered-answer accuracy from 54% to 69% as coverage fell.

  • RadOnc-Agent: RadOnc-Agent: An LLM-Orchestrated Framework for AI Workflows Across the Radiotherapy Care Pathway
    2026-10 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    An LLM controller maps clinical intent to 26 schema-constrained function calls across four radiotherapy phases while keeping patient and workflow state. It chose the intended function in 98.8 percent of 7,800 single-function executions and completed about 96.5 percent of cross-stage and real-patient workflows, with longitudinal state removal cutting completion to 84 percent.

  • Orchestrator: Orchestrator multi-agent clinical decision support system for secondary headache diagnosis in primary care
    2026-10 · Journal of the American Medical Informatics Association : JAMIA · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    The Orchestrator system uses a multi-agent architecture to assist in the diagnosis of secondary headaches by breaking down clinical reasoning into specialized agents, each focusing on specific red flag domains. The study demonstrated that this approach enhances diagnostic accuracy and transparency compared to single large language model methods.

  • PMxAgent: PMxAgent: An Agentic Platform for Pharmacometrics
    2026-10 · CPT: pharmacometrics & systems pharmacology · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Hybrid; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    PMxAgent is an open-source platform that automates pharmacometric analyses by integrating various tools into a Docker-based API server, enabling AI agents to perform tasks like non-compartmental analysis and exposure-response modeling. The system was benchmarked against other AI agents for accuracy and reproducibility in NCA workflows, demonstrating superior performance and deterministic results.

  • StrokeAgent: An auditable multimodal agentic system for sequential decision-making across the acute stroke pathway
    2026-10 · medRxiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    StrokeAgent is a multimodal agentic system designed to support decision-making in acute stroke care by integrating clinical records, laboratory results, ECG waveforms, and CT images. The system demonstrated high concordance with expert decisions and was rated highly for clinical utility and reasoning quality compared to large language models and neurologists.

  • End-to-End PET/CT Interpretation and Quantification with an LLM-Orchestrated AI Agent: A Real-World Pilot Study
    2026-10 · Journal of nuclear medicine : official publication, Society of Nuclear Medicine · Clinical decision support; Single-agent, Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    The system uses a large language model to orchestrate an end-to-end workflow for PET/CT image interpretation and report generation, demonstrating high sensitivity in primary tumor detection but lower specificity in nodal involvement and distant metastasis assessment.

  • CXRAgent: CXRAgent: Director-Orchestrated Multi-Stage Reasoning for Chest X-Ray Interpretation
    2026-10 · IEEE transactions on medical imaging · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    CXRAgent has a central director that orchestrates chest X-ray analysis tools, validates their outputs against visual evidence, plans the next diagnostic step and reasons over collected findings. It is built to adapt to new diagnostic tasks and to weigh how reliable each tool is.

  • Audiologist-Guided Multimodal AI for Pure-Tone Audiometry and Tympanometry Interpretation and Reporting
    2026-10 · Journal of medical systems · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    The paper describes a multimodal AI system that assists audiologists in interpreting and reporting pure-tone audiometry and tympanometry results. The study evaluates the accuracy of the AI's performance in different modules, showing improvements in agreement rates for hearing loss types and acoustic reflexes when guided by specific skills or prompts.

  • EHR-RobustGym: EHR-RobustGym: Benchmarking and Training Agents for Robust Clinical Reasoning
    2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented

    EHR-RobustGym is a scalable and interactive environment designed to evaluate and train clinical agents on noisy electronic health records, aiming to improve their robustness in real-world scenarios. The system reveals significant performance drops when agents are exposed to noise, indicating the need for more robust training methods.

  • Design and implementation of an intelligent ICD coding system using large language model
    2026-09 · Research Square · Clinical decision support; Single-agent, Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    The paper describes an automated ICD coding system that uses a large language model and a multi-agent framework to assign diagnostic and procedural codes accurately. The system was evaluated using real-world medical records, demonstrating high precision and recall rates for both diagnosis and procedure codes.

  • STAM: Personalized State-Transition-Aware Memory for Clinical Agents
    2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric

    STAM is a memory framework for clinical agents that tracks patient state transitions while preserving historical context. It demonstrated improved performance in downstream tasks and diagnostics compared to baseline methods across four longitudinal clinical benchmarks.

  • KlinikeBench: KlinikeBench: Evaluating Language Models Beyond Diagnostic Accuracy
    2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Human-in-the-loop, Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    KlinikeBench evaluates language models in clinical settings beyond diagnostic accuracy by simulating full patient interactions, including history gathering and examination requests. The benchmark reveals a significant gap between diagnosis accuracy and interactive performance, with professional clinicians rating model-generated dialogues higher than real conversations.

  • PrivMeSA: PrivMeSA: Privacy-Aware Self-Evolving Multi-Agent System for Medicine via Local-Remote LLM Collaboration
    2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    PrivMeSA is a privacy-aware multi-agent system designed to manage medical consultations while minimizing patient data exposure. It uses reinforcement learning to balance task accuracy with privacy risks, demonstrating significant improvements in both clinical decision-making and privacy protection compared to direct remote consultation methods.

  • A Multi-Agent Framework for Explainable Brain Tumour Instance Segmentation and Clinical Report Generation Using ASIO-Optimised YOLO Architectures
    2026-09 · Research Square · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    The paper presents a multi-agent framework for brain tumor instance segmentation and clinical report generation using YOLO architectures optimized with ASIO. The system demonstrates improved diagnostic precision and rapid inference latency, outperforming baseline methods in terms of mean Average Precision.

  • Unknown is not normal: separating language-model extraction from rule-based decision logic for clinical risk scores
    2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent, Neuro-symbolic; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    An LLM marks each finding present, absent or unknown while deterministic code computes score bounds and asks only the questions that could change the risk category, across six clinical calculators. On 1,200 synthetic emergency cases it matched asking everything with half the questions, while treating missing as normal under-triaged 8.5 percent of patients and an end-to-end agent asked irrelevant questions.

  • CLEAR-Med: Large Language Models for Structured Clinical Data Analysis: Dual-Agent Grounding and Validation
    2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support, Multi-omics analysis; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    CLEAR-Med splits natural-language analysis of structured clinical data into an invocation agent that writes and runs SQL and a separate cross-provider validation agent that accepts, requests one repair, or abstains. On a 21-site neonatal encephalopathy table of 532 infants, the invocation agent answered 66.4% of benchmark queries correctly versus 12.0% ungrounded.

  • ECG-Scroll: ECG-Scroll: A Long-Horizon, Streaming Benchmark and Agent Environment for Interpretation of Ambulatory Electrocardiograms
    2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    ECG-Scroll streams long ambulatory ECG recordings to an agent chunk by chunk so it must decide what to measure, store evidence as it passes and flag paroxysmal events without seeing the future signal. Because the signal is kept, answers are checked against objective ground truth with rule-based rewards.

  • Multi-agent LLM diagnostic teams improve differential recall on curated cases and reverse on real emergency notes: a label-level reversal without a clinician-judged safety difference
    2026-09 · medRxiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop

    Compares a single LLM call, five personas in one context and five blinded specialist agents merged by a moderator on curated case collections and 364 real emergency notes. The moderated team improved top-3 recall on curated cases but lowered it on real emergency notes, with blinded physicians finding no difference in error danger.

  • MACBT: MACBT: A Multi-Agent Cognitive Behavioral Therapy Decision Support System with Longitudinal Memory
    2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    MACBT is a multi-agent system designed to support cognitive behavioral therapy (CBT) for depression, aiding clinicians in session preparation and documentation. The system includes a longitudinal memory module that tracks patient progress over time, demonstrating improved professionalism and clinical authenticity compared to existing systems.

  • SkinAgent AI: SkinAgent AI: A Safety-Grounded Multimodal Agentic Framework for Non-Diagnostic Skincare Support
    2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    SkinAgent AI is a multimodal framework that uses visual evidence and product information to provide skincare support, ensuring safety and privacy. The system demonstrated high accuracy in skin condition routing and skin-type estimation but showed limitations in tool selection and task completion.

  • DrGait: DrGait: Biomechanically Grounded Visual Reasoning for Interpretable Clinical Gait Analysis
    2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent, Neuro-symbolic; Step-by-step; Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented, Tool-augmented

    DrGait is a training-free framework that uses Vision-Language Models to analyze gait videos for clinical applications, ensuring interpretability and accuracy by decoupling semantic reasoning from geometric perception through a structured workflow. It demonstrates the ability to generate transparent and audit-ready clinical reports while maintaining competitive diagnostic accuracy.

  • Eli5a v2.0: Comparative Evaluation of a New Autonomous AI Agent Versus Frontier LLMs for AI-Generated Patient Information Sheets on Pediatric Pathologies
    2026-09 · Cancers · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented

    Eli5a v2.0 is an autonomous AI agent designed to generate patient information sheets for pediatric oral pathologies and rare head and neck tumors. The study found that Eli5a excelled in understandability, reading grade level, and actionability compared to other LLMs, highlighting its potential in creating accessible educational materials for complex medical conditions.

  • TrialAtlas: TrialAtlas: Multi-Agent Research Organization for Clinical Trial Design and Optimization
    2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented

    TrialAtlas is a multi-agent system designed to assist in clinical trial design and optimization by synthesizing literature, analyzing regulatory precedents, and providing integrated reasoning. It demonstrated superior performance compared to baselines in predicting the success of clinical trials and detecting deficiencies, as validated through both automated benchmarks and expert evaluations.

  • A Modular Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning Framework Guided by Large Language Models: Improving Quality and Efficiency of Treatment Planning in Lung Radiation Therapy
    2026-09 · International journal of radiation oncology, biology, physics · Clinical decision support; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    A two-level framework uses LLM planner, evaluator and supervisor agents to coordinate reinforcement-learning agents that adjust doses in lung IMRT treatment planning. On 62 retrospective lung cancer cases it improved plan constraint compliance and efficiency relative to knowledge-based planning.

  • SAFE-AI: A medically grounded LLM agent-based tool to detect patient safety events in medical records
    2026-09 · PLOS digital health · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented

    SAFE-AI is a tool that uses an ontology-driven model to minimize hallucinations in large language models (LLMs) for detecting patient safety events, specifically medication errors, in medical records.     2026-09 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric

  • Improving Automated Deep Phenotyping Through Large Language Models Using Retrieval Augmented Generation †
    2025-08 · Genome Medicine · Clinical decision support; Single-agent, Neuro-symbolic; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    This paper introduces RAG-HPO, a retrieval-augmented generation tool that improves automatic extraction and assignment of Human Phenotype Ontology (HPO) terms from clinical text. By combining LLM-based phrase extraction with real-time retrieval from a large vector database of phenotype–HPO mappings, RAG-HPO reduces hallucinations and avoids costly fine-tuning. Evaluated on 112 rare-disease case reports, it significantly outperforms existing HPO extraction tools in precision, recall, and F1 score, with most false positives being clinically relevant ancestor terms rather than hallucinations. The results demonstrate that RAG-based grounding enables accurate, reliable phenotype extraction for clinical genomics and rare disease diagnosis.

  • Benchmarking Large Language Models for Replication of Guideline-Based PGx Recommendations †
    2025-07 · The Pharmacogenomics Journal · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    This study assesses how well large language models can generate clinically accurate pharmacogenomic (PGx) recommendations aligned with CPIC guidelines. Using 599 curated gene–drug–phenotype scenarios and a novel expert-validated semantic metric (LLM Score), the authors show that general-purpose LLMs often produce incomplete or unsafe recommendations. In contrast, a domain-adapted, fine-tuned model achieves substantially better accuracy (LLM Score = 0.92) and faster inference. The results emphasize that fine-tuning and structured prompting are more critical than model scale alone for safe, effective AI-driven personalized medicine.

  • Survey and Improvement Strategies for Gene Prioritization with Large Language Models †
    2025 · Bioinformatics Advances · Clinical decision support; Single-agent, Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric

    This study benchmarks large language models for gene prioritization in rare disease diagnosis, addressing challenges from limited data and large candidate gene sets. Using multi-agent strategies, Human Phenotype Ontology–based case stratification, and a divide-and-conquer ranking framework, GPT-4 consistently outperformed other models in identifying causal genes while mitigating positional and literature biases. Although biases toward well-studied genes and input order sensitivity remain, the proposed framework significantly improves diagnostic efficiency for rare genetic disorders. This work proposes an autonomous mechanical design framework that combines a pretrained large language model with finite element method (FEM) analysis to iteratively generate, evaluate, and refine structural designs without domain-specific fine-tuning. Using 2D truss structures as a testbed, the LLM effectively navigates discrete, multi-objective design spaces and achieves faster convergence with fewer FEM evaluations than NSGA-II. The results demonstrate that reasoning-based, language-driven LLMs can serve as efficient optimizers for autonomous structural design and iterative refinement. -->

  • Boosting GPT Models for Genomics Analysis: Generating Trusted Genetic Variant Annotations and Interpretations through RAG and fine-tuning †
    2025 · Bioinformatics Advances · Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    This study aims to enhance large language model performance in genomics by incorporating large-scale variant annotation data using retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) and fine-tuning. By integrating 190 million curated variant annotations from five major datasets into GPT-4o, the system enables accurate, reasoning-supported variant queries and interpretations. Results show that while fine-tuning improves performance in some annotation fields, RAG is more effective overall in terms of scalability, accuracy, and cost-efficiency for injecting factual genomic knowledge. The work demonstrates the potential of LLMs as accessible and efficient tools for variant interpretation and specialized genomics applications.

  • Leveraging Generative AI to Assist Biocuration of Medical Actions for Rare Disease †
    2024-08 · Bioinformatics Advances · Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Neuro-symbolic, Single-agent; Hybrid; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented

    This paper presents AutoMAxO, a semi-automated LLM-based workflow designed to scale the biocuration of the Medical Action Ontology (MAxO) for rare diseases, addressing the limitations of manual curation. The system uses LLMs to extract candidate treatment and medical action annotations from literature abstracts, then aligns them with MAxO, HPO, and MONDO ontology terms through post-processing and human-in-the-loop validation. Applied to abstracts covering 37 rare genetic diseases, AutoMAxO identified 958 novel treatment annotations incorporated into the MAxO dataset. The results demonstrate that LLM-assisted curation can substantially improve coverage and efficiency in clinical ontology development.

2024 (29)

Clinical decision support

  • Attitudes Toward Use of an APOL1 Genetic Testing Chatbot in Living Kidney Donor Evaluation: A Focus Group Study †
    2024-12 · Clinical Transplantation · Clinical decision support; Human-in-the-loop; Knowledge-augmented

    This study explores attitudes toward using the Gia® genetic information chatbot to support APOL1 testing among living kidney donor candidates of African ancestry. Through focus groups and surveys involving donors, recipients, and community members, participants generally supported using the chatbot prior to clinical visits and expressed interest in APOL1 testing, though concerns about cost, personal preferences, and individual barriers emerged. Overall, participants were receptive to integrating the APOL1 chatbot into the donor evaluation workflow. The findings suggest that culturally adapted chatbots can facilitate genetic testing discussions and integration in clinical care.

  • OpenAI o1-Preview vs. ChatGPT in Healthcare: A New Frontier in Medical AI Reasoning †
    2024-10 · Cureus · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner

    This editorial compares OpenAI o1-Preview with ChatGPT-4 in healthcare applications, highlighting advances in reasoning capabilities and their implications for clinical decision support. The work discusses opportunities and limitations of next-generation generative AI models in medical reasoning contexts.

  • Approximating facial expression effects on diagnostic accuracy via generative AI in medical genetics †
    2024-06 · Bioinformatics · Clinical decision support; Automated, model-centric, Human-in-the-loop

    This study examines how facial expressions influence AI and human diagnosis of genetic syndromes using facial images. Both deep learning models and clinicians were more likely to correctly identify Williams and Angelman syndromes when individuals were smiling, revealing a bias linked to stereotypical expressions, while no such effect was observed for other syndromes. The findings highlight facial expression as an important confounder that should be considered and mitigated in AI driven genomic and clinical diagnostics.

  • Evolution of publicly available large language models for complex decision-making in breast cancer care †
    2024-05 · Archives of Gynecology and Obstetrics · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Human-in-the-loop

    This study compares the treatment recommendations of five publicly available large language models (GPT-4, GPT-3.5 variants, Llama2, and Bard) with those of a multidisciplinary tumor board for complex breast cancer cases. GPT-4 showed the highest concordance, particularly for invasive breast cancer and radiotherapy decisions, while all models performed poorly in genetic testing recommendations. Overall, the findings indicate that current LLMs are not yet sufficiently reliable for safe clinical use in breast cancer care, highlighting the need for improved prompting strategies, data control, and further clinical validation.

  • A Comparative Evaluation of ChatGPT 3.5 and ChatGPT 4 in Responses to Selected Genetics Questions †
    2024-05 · Journal of the American Medical Informatics Association · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Human-in-the-loop

    This study evaluates GPT-4’s ability to deliver genetic information on BRCA1, HFE, and MLH1 compared with GPT-3.5 using expert review by genetic counselors and clinicians. GPT-4 showed significantly improved accuracy and generally favorable relevance, but persistent errors and usability limitations remained. The findings highlight GPT-4’s promise in genetic education while emphasizing the need for continued refinement and ethical oversight in clinical use.

  • Innovations in Medicine: Exploring ChatGPT’s Impact on Rare Disorder Management †
    2024-03 · Genes · Clinical decision support

    This review examines the role of AI, particularly ChatGPT, in advancing research, education, and clinical practice for rare and genetic disorders. It highlights the potential of AI to improve personalized patient care while critically discussing current limitations and risks. The study outlines future directions for developing AI tools that can better support healthcare professionals and patients in rare disease management.

  • Assessing the Utility of Large Language Models for Phenotype-Driven Gene Prioritization in Rare Genetic Disorder Diagnosis †
    2024-03 · The American Journal of Human Genetics · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    This study evaluates multiple large language models for phenotype driven gene prioritization in rare disease diagnosis across different prompts, input types, and task settings. Although GPT 4 performed best, its accuracy remained substantially lower than that of traditional gene prioritization tools, with limited benefit from retrieval augmentation or few shot learning. The findings highlight both the potential and current limitations of LLMs in clinical genomic workflows, including biases toward well studied genes.

  • Tokensome: Towards a Genetic Vision-Language GPT for Explainable and Cognitive Karyotyping †
    2024-03 · arXiv · Clinical decision support; Neuro-symbolic; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    This paper argues that existing automatic karyotype analysis methods are limited by treating the task purely as visual object recognition, neglecting holistic information and interpretability needed for clinical use. The authors introduce Tokensome, a vision–language model that represents chromosomes through tokenization to enable explainable, cognitively driven karyotyping. By elevating analysis from visual perception to cognitive decision-making, Tokensome integrates domain knowledge and reasoning via knowledge graphs and LLMs. This approach improves interpretability and enhances the detection of chromosomal abnormalities.

  • GeneGPT: Augmenting Large Language Models with Domain Tools for Improved Access to Biomedical Information †
    2024-02 · Bioinformatics · Clinical decision support, Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    This work presents GeneGPT, a framework that enables large language models to answer genomics questions by directly invoking NCBI Web APIs. By learning to generate and execute API calls through in-context learning and augmented decoding, GeneGPT substantially reduces hallucinations and achieves state-of-the-art performance on the GeneTuring benchmark. The results demonstrate that tool-augmented LLMs can reliably perform complex and multi-hop reasoning in genomics tasks.

  • Advancing Healthcare: The Role and Impact of AI and Foundation Models †
    2024-01 · American Journal of Translational Research · Clinical decision support

    This review examines the evolution of foundation AI models in healthcare, highlighting their growing impact on diagnostics, medical interventions, and precision medicine across domains such as imaging, genomics, and decision support. It discusses both the transformative potential of generative and traditional AI models and the challenges of slow adoption, ethics, bias, and governance in healthcare systems. The authors advocate for transparent, fair, and accountable AI frameworks, including the concept of General Medical AI, to enable scalable and responsible healthcare innovation.

Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation

  • Steering veridical large language model analyses by correcting and enriching generated database queries: first steps toward ChatGPT bioinformatics †
    2024-11 · Briefings in Bioinformatics · Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation, Multi-omics analysis; Multi-agent, Neuro-symbolic; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented, Knowledge-augmented

    This paper examines the limitations of using plain ChatGPT as a bioinformatics assistant, highlighting errors caused by incomplete knowledge, poor data retrieval, and hallucinations in scientific domains. To address these issues, the authors introduce NagGPT, a middleware that sits between LLMs and genomics databases to validate, correct, and mediate LLM-generated queries while guiding responses with authoritative, up-to-date data. Paired with a custom GPT that generates and executes Python code, the system enables dynamic retrieval and analysis across major genomics databases. The results demonstrate practical ways to improve factual accuracy, reduce hallucinations, and enhance the reliability of unmodified LLMs for bioinformatics tasks.

  • RNA-GPT: Multimodal Generative System for RNA Sequence Understanding †
    2024-10 · arXiv · Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    This paper introduces **RNA-GPT**, a multimodal RNA-focused language model designed to support RNA research by integrating RNA sequence encoders with large language models. RNA-GPT aligns RNA sequence representations with textual knowledge, enabling it to process user-provided RNA sequences and answer complex RNA-related queries accurately. The system is trained using RNA-QA, an automated pipeline that extracts and organizes RNA annotations from RNACentral to generate large-scale instruction-tuning data. Experiments show that RNA-GPT effectively facilitates RNA discovery, supported by a curated dataset of over 400,000 RNA samples for modality alignment and instruction tuning.

  • GP-GPT: Large Language Model for Gene-phenotype Mapping †
    2024-09 · arXiv · Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric

    This work introduces GP-GPT, a specialized large language model fine-tuned on large scale genomics, proteomics, and medical genetics data for genetic–phenotype knowledge representation and relation analysis. GP-GPT outperforms general purpose LLMs on genomics information retrieval and relationship determination tasks, demonstrating strong domain specific understanding. The results suggest that tailored LLMs can more effectively support gene–phenotype research and biomedical knowledge discovery.

  • VarChat: the generative AI assistant for the interpretation of human genomic variations †
    2024-03 · Bioinformatics · Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation, Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    This work introduces VarChat, a generative-AI–based tool designed to address the growing challenge of genomic variant interpretation amid rapidly expanding scientific literature. VarChat automatically identifies, synthesizes, and summarizes variant-related research into concise, clinically relevant descriptions, linking genetic variants to protein function and health outcomes while providing traceable references. By streamlining literature review and interpretation, VarChat helps translate fragmented genomic knowledge into actionable insights for researchers and clinicians.

  • ChIP-GPT: a managed large language model for robust data extraction from biomedical database records †
    2024-01 · Briefings in Bioinformatics · Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric

    ChIP-GPT presents a fine-tuned LLM system for scalable and robust metadata extraction from biomedical databases. By iteratively prompting a customized GPT model, the framework accurately extracts ChIP-seq targets and cell lines from noisy or incomplete records, enabling expert-level reasoning over large-scale database entries.

  • FAVOR-GPT: A Generative Natural Language Interface to Whole Genome Variant Functional Annotations †
    2024-01 · Bioinformatics Advances · Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    This work introduces FAVOR-GPT, a retrieval augmented generative interface that integrates large language models with the FAVOR database to support interpretation of whole genome variant annotations. By translating complex functional annotations into user friendly summaries, FAVOR-GPT improves accessibility and usability for researchers without specialized expertise. The results demonstrate that RAG based LLM interfaces can effectively enhance genomic data interpretation while maintaining high annotation accuracy.

  • Evaluating GPT and BERT Models for Protein-Protein Interaction Identification in Biomedical Text †
    2024-01 · Bioinformatics Advances · Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric

    This study evaluates transformer-based language models for extracting protein–protein interactions from biomedical text. Bidirectional encoder models, especially BioBERT, achieved the strongest overall performance, while GPT-4 demonstrated comparable precision and recall despite not being specifically trained on biomedical corpora. The results indicate that large language models can effectively support automated PPI mining from the biomedical literature.

    2024-10 · arXiv · Molecular & drug design; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    Designing transition metal complexes involves searching an enormous chemical space, which limits the effectiveness of traditional genetic algorithms. This work introduces an LLM driven evolutionary optimization framework that leverages pretrained chemical knowledge to guide single and multi objective optimization without supervised fine tuning. The proposed approach efficiently identifies top performing complexes, enables flexible natural language driven optimization, and demonstrates strong potential for chemistry and materials design.

  • CancerGPT for few shot drug pair synergy prediction using large pretrained language models †
    2024-02 · npj Digital Medicine · Molecular & drug design; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric

    Large language models show strong few-shot learning ability, but their capacity to generalize to complex biological problems remains underexplored. This study proposes a few-shot LLM-based approach for predicting drug pair synergy in rare cancer tissues by leveraging prior knowledge from text rather than structured data. Experiments across seven rare tissues demonstrate that the compact CancerGPT model achieves high accuracy with minimal or no samples, performing comparably to much larger fine-tuned GPT-3 models.

  • BioDiscoveryAgent: An AI Agent for Designing Genetic Perturbation Experiments †
    2024-05 · arXiv · Multi-omics analysis; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented, Knowledge-augmented

    BioDiscoveryAgent is an LLM-based autonomous agent that designs and evaluates genetic perturbation experiments by reasoning over biological knowledge without requiring explicit optimization objectives or model training. Across multiple datasets, it substantially outperforms Bayesian optimization baselines in identifying relevant genes and gene combinations, including challenging non-essential perturbation tasks and unseen data. By integrating literature search, data analysis, and self-critique, BioDiscoveryAgent offers an interpretable and efficient paradigm for closed-loop biological experiment design.

  • PheNormGPT: a framework for extraction and normalization of key medical findings †
    2024-01 · Database · Multi-omics analysis, Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric

Using fine-tuned GPT models and a novel adaptive few-shot selection strategy, PheNormGPT achieved state-of-the-art performance in the BioCreative VIII phenotype extraction challenge.   • CellAgent: An LLM-driven MultiAgent Framework for Automated Single-cell Data Analysis †
    2024 · arXiv · Multi-omics analysis; Multi-agent; Step-by-step; Automated, model-centric; Tool-augmented

    This paper introduces CellAgent, an LLM-driven multi-agent framework that fully automates single-cell RNA-seq data analysis by coordinating planner, executor, and evaluator agents with hierarchical decision making and self-iterative optimization. CellAgent autonomously selects appropriate tools and hyperparameters, executes end-to-end analyses, and evaluates results without human intervention. Benchmarks across diverse tissues and cell types show that CellAgent achieves robust, high-quality performance while substantially reducing analyst workload, advancing an “Agent for Science” paradigm.

2023 (15)

Clinical decision support

  • Leveraging Large Language Models for Decision Support in Personalized Oncology †
    2023-11 · JAMA Network Open · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Human-in-the-loop

    This study evaluates four large language models as assistants in precision oncology by comparing their treatment recommendations with those of human experts. While the models produced more candidate treatment options, their agreement with expert decisions was low due to limited supporting evidence, though some clinically useful and unique suggestions emerged. The findings suggest LLMs may support idea generation and literature screening but cannot replace expert judgment.

  • ChatGPT for phenotypes extraction: one model to rule them all? †
    2023-07 · IEEE EMBC · Clinical decision support; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric; Knowledge-augmented

    This study examines whether large language models (LLMs) can capture the full spectrum of human reasoning, including both fast, intuitive System 1 and slow, deliberative System 2 processes. Using a generalized Natural Language Inference task and crowdsourced human response distributions, the authors evaluate LLMs’ ability to reproduce not just majority answers but the full diversity of human judgments. They introduce personality-based prompting inspired by the Big Five model, optimized with genetic algorithms, to elicit diverse reasoning styles from LLMs. Results show that this approach significantly improves alignment with human response distributions, with open-source models outperforming proprietary ones, suggesting that modeling personality and suboptimal reasoning is key to more human-like AI cognition.

  • Can Large Language Models Democratize Access to Dual-use Biotechnology? †
    2023-06 · Arxiv · Clinical decision support, Molecular & drug design, Exploratory applications; Single-agent; Step-by-step; Human-in-the-loop

    Large language models are accelerating and democratizing research by providing accessible expertise across disciplines, but they also risk lowering barriers to dual use technologies with serious biosecurity implications. An educational experiment showed that, with minimal prompting, chatbots could guide non experts through key steps related to pandemic scale agents, indicating that such capabilities may become broadly accessible. These findings highlight the urgent need for safeguards such as third party model evaluation, careful curation of training data, and comprehensive DNA synthesis screening to mitigate misuse risks.

    2023-09 · ACS synthetic biology · Knowledge discovery & hypothesis generation; Single-agent; Zero-shot planner; Automated, model-centric

    This work demonstrates how GPT-4–based prompt engineering can streamline knowledge extraction from synthetic biology literature. By mining 176 publications on oleaginous yeasts, the authors converted unstructured text into structured datasets that enabled machine-learning models to accurately predict fermentation performance. The results show that generative AI can significantly reduce manual curation effort, support predictive modeling in biomanufacturing, and enable transfer learning to less-studied microbial systems.

Across six representative tasks—including palladium-catalyzed reaction optimization—Coscientist demonstrates strong capabilities in semi-autonomous experimental design, execution, and reasoning.
